Title: Epistolae HN. The Principall Epistles of HN, which he hath set-foorth through the holy Spirit of Loue and written and sent them most-cheeflie vnto the Louers of the Trueth and his Acquaintance. And are by him newly perused, and more-playnly declared
Creator: Hendrik Niclaes (H. N.), founder of the Family of Love (translator from the Low German not named: "Translated out of Base-almaine")
Publisher: The host catalogues the printer as N. Bohmberg (Cologne), 1575; no imprint is legible in the OCR
Publication Date: 1575
Rights: Public Domain (published 1575; public domain by date)
Source: Internet Archive item bim_early-english-books-1475-1640_epistole-hn-the-princip_niclas-hendrik_1575, file bim_early-english-books-1475-1640_epistole-hn-the-princip_niclas-hendrik_1575_djvu.txt, fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, one-world agent for family-of-love-and-spiritualists-franck-niclaes), 2026-10-16; host rights statement: none on the item (no possible-copyright-status and no licence field; collections pub_early-english-books-*, bim_microfilm; an EEBO microfilm scan contributed by Internet Archive, not access-restricted).
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged apart from long-s to s normalisation (11477 occurrences). Rights basis restated: a sixteenth-century print (host date 1575; the OCR shows no imprint year) is public domain by date; no modern editorial text is in the file.
Content note: The whole print: title page, then twenty numbered epistles, each in chapters, each closing with a line "The Ende of the N. Epistle" (the OCR reads "The Firste Epistle" at file line 505 and the closing lines of the first, second, third, fourth, sixth to ninth, eleventh and thirteenth to twentieth epistles; "The Ende of the twentith Epistle" at line 23306). Niclaes's letters to his followers and enquirers, the body of his pastoral and prophetic voice (the separately printed Epistola XI, "Correctio", is Epistle XI here). SECOND WITNESS for exact wording: EEBO microfilm OCR of a black-letter print, sampled at five points and found readable (about 0.86 of tokens read as words) but with the systematic black-letter misreads noted for the Terra Pacis file ("ꝛ" for r, "/" virgules, broken words); check quotations against the page images.
